# C60: a nonlinear two-state legal trajectory with dense turning gradients

Status: **new explicit construction and scoped calculations; UNVERIFIED**.
No solve or verifier call is used. The numerical allocation is closed.
This branch follows the finite C59 reconstruction by seeking an analytic
correlated core. It does not assume that the C59 data belong to this family.

The construction works for every finite nonnegative schedule, including
zeros and arbitrarily large entries, with one fixed globally convex
1-smooth function. Its all-pair law and true closest-minimizer distance
are explicit. The quantitative payoff on the critical H_m remains open.

## 1. The global function, its gradients, and its minimizers

In R^2 let

    C_par={(t,s): s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},
    F_par(X)=sup_(g in C_par) [<X,g>-||g||^2/2].      (1)

The set is closed, convex and nonempty, and contains0. Completing the
square shows that the maximizer in (1) is the Euclidean projection of X
onto C_par. It exists and is unique because the squared-distance objective
is coercive and strictly convex. Thus F_par is finite and convex.

For completeness write p(X) for that projection. Its elementary normal
condition is

    <X-p(X),g-p(X)><=0 for every g in C_par.

Applying this twice and adding gives

    ||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>.

Thus p is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizers in (1) gives

    0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>
        <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.

Consequently F_par is differentiable, its gradient is p, and it is
1-smooth. This proof supplies the needed global property directly.

The minimizers are exactly

    argmin F_par={(0,y):y<=0}.                      (2)

Indeed F_par>=0 by the feasible vector0. For X=(0,y), y<=0, every g=(t,s)
in C_par has <X,g>=ys<=0, so the maximum in (1) is0. Conversely, if
the vertical coordinate y>0, taking g=(0,s) with small s>0 makes the
objective positive. If the horizontal coordinate x is nonzero and y<=0,
take g=(t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with sufficiently small nonzero t of the sign of x:
the linear term xt dominates the terms of quadratic and quartic order.
This again makes the objective positive. These cases prove (2).

This F_par is not a convex quadratic: its minimizer set is a ray, whereas
the nonempty minimizer set of an unconstrained convex quadratic is an
affine subspace.

## 2. Coordinates that make every interpolation pair explicit

For a real r and v>=0, put

    u=v+r^2,
    X(r,v)=(r(1+v+r^2), -v/sqrt(2)),
    G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).                           (3)

Then X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any (t,s) in C_par,

    <X-G,(t,s)-G>
      =u[r(t-r)-(s-r^2/sqrt(2))/sqrt(2)]
      <=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0.

The normal condition therefore proves that G(r) is the actual gradient
of F_par at X(r,v). Direct substitution into (1) gives

    F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                (4)

For any two sampled states r_i,v_i and r_j,v_j, with the actual values
from (4), the complete signed interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>
        =-(u_j/2)(r_i-r_j)^2<=0.                   (5)

The first equality after Q follows by substituting
f_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2. The second uses the displayed parabolic
coordinates. This proves EVERY ordered pair simultaneously for one
function; no local, adjacent, or cycle relaxation is being used.

For an initial nonzero r_0 and v_0>=0, the closest minimizer in (2) is

    X_star=(0,-v_0/sqrt(2)),
    R_0^2=dist(X_0,argmin F_par)^2
            =r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2.                (6)

The optimum sample fits the same formulas with r_star=0, v_star=v_0,
u_star=v_0. Thus (5) includes both of its directions. Choosing the origin
instead would generally overestimate the true initial distance; (6) is
the normalization used throughout.

## 3. The exact GD transition, with no interpolation optimization

For ANY step h>=0, starting from state(r,v), define

    v_plus=v+h r^2,
    r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus
                      =r(1+v+r^2-h).              (7)

The cubic's left side is strictly increasing from minus infinity to
plus infinity because its derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1. Thus (7)
defines exactly one real r_plus, by an explicit scalar algebraic rule.
There is no nearest-feasible-trajectory problem hidden in this definition.

The vertical coordinate of X-hG is -v_plus/sqrt(2), and its horizontal
coordinate is the right side of (7). Hence

    X(r_plus,v_plus)=X(r,v)-h G(r).

This proves actual GD for the same global function. In particular h=0
leaves the state unchanged, and every finite positive step gives finite
new state. All v_i remain nonnegative, regardless of gradient sign changes.

For a supplied finite nonnegative schedule h_0,...,h_(n-1), iterate (7)
from any r_0!=0,v_0>=0. Its exact normalized payoff is

    P_par(h;r_0,v_0)
       =[r_n^2(1+v_n)+(3/2)r_n^4]
                        /[r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2].  (8)

The function (1), all values(4), gradients(3), positions(3), and initial
distance(6) are fixed together. Thus (8) is an actual lower witness for
W_n(h), albeit not yet a profile-strength one on the critical schedules.

Some immediate identities expose the changing geometry:

    u_plus-u=(h-1)r^2+r_plus^2,
    X_plus,horizontal=X_horizontal[1-h/(1+u)].      (9)

In particular, for schedules with all h>=1, the normal parameter u is
nondecreasing, but r and the horizontal gradient can turn repeatedly.
The Hessian direction changes with r; this is not a fixed linear spectral
transcript. The vertical state accumulates h r^2 rather than discarding
previous turns.

## 4. Exact dense-jump calibration on a different schedule

Take any sigma in(0,1), v_0>=0, and define

    U_0=1+v_0+sigma,
    rho=(1+sigma)/(1-sigma),
    h_i=2U_0 rho^i/(1-sigma),
    r_i=(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),
    v_i=U_0 rho^i-1-sigma.                         (10)

These v_i are nonnegative. Substitution into (7) gives
v_(i+1)-v_i=h_i sigma and
r_(i+1)(1+v_(i+1)+sigma)=r_i(1+v_i+sigma-h_i),
so (10) is an exact trajectory of F_par. Its payoff is

    P_par=rho^n/U_0+sigma/(2U_0^2).                 (11)

This calibration is legal and can have large payoff. It is a geometric
step sequence, NOT the critical ruler word, so (11) proves no claim about
H_m or the original profile. It only checks that the mechanism supports
correlated turns without a feasibility repair.

There are n nonzero collinear gradient jumps in (10):

    G_i-G_(i-1)=(2(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),0), 1<=i<=n.

Their Gram J has trace4sigma n and twice the absolute off-diagonal sum
4sigma n(n-1). For any Gram realizable with at most k nonzero jumps in
each orthonormal coordinate, Cauchy--Schwarz coordinate by coordinate
implies2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|<=(k-1)trace(J). Hence this trajectory requires
some coordinate with at least n jumps in every orthonormal realization.
Dense chronological correlation does not require a large ambient dimension.

For a completely rational parameter example, sigma=1/4 and v_0=0 give
rho=5/3, h_0=10/3, h_1=50/9, and r_i=(-1)^i/2. Formula (11) is then
an exact expression, not a numerical simulation.

## 5. Two simple parameter limits do not yet give the desired mechanism

First fix a finite schedule and fix v_0=A>=0. Let r_0=t tend to zero.
Induction in (7) gives

    v_i=A+O(t^2),
    r_i=t product_(j<i)[1-h_j/(1+A)]+O(t^3).

At each induction step, the inverse cubic has nonzero derivative1+A
at zero, and its cubic term is O(t^3), giving the displayed expansion.
Thus the payoff tends to the ordinary scalar quadratic value

    [1/(1+A)] product_(j<n)[1-h_j/(1+A)]^2.         (12)

The implicit constants here depend on the entire fixed finite schedule.
They have NOT been bounded uniformly along growing H_m. Consequently
(12) is a calibration and an explanation of why an infinitesimal core
does not itself add a new mechanism; it is not a class obstruction.

Second put S=sum h_i and sigma=r_0^2. If v_0>=S, no horizontal sign flip
occurs and |r_(i+1)|<=|r_i|. Indeed h_i<=v_i and the right side of (7)
has the sign of r_i. Evaluating the increasing cubic at r_i shows that
its excess over that right side is h_i r_i(1+r_i^2), with the appropriate
sign, so the claimed decrease in absolute value follows. Hence

    v_n<=v_0+S sigma,
    P_par <= [1+v_0+S sigma+(3/2)sigma]
                                  /(1+v_0+sigma)^2. (13)

Along any growing-horizon choices with
v_0/[S(1+sigma)] tending to infinity, this gives S P_par tending to zero.
Thus placing the initial state arbitrarily far down the flat minimizer
direction also loses the desired scale. This rules out only the displayed
parameter regime; intermediate coupled regimes remain open.

## 6. The natural geometric calibration is small on the entire critical word

One tempting choice is to match the geometric growth ratio rho in(10)
to the physical critical parameter c_m. This means

    m>=128, epsilon=1/m,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
    sigma=(c-1)/(c+1), r_0=sqrt(sigma), v_0=0.       (14)

For THIS particular starting rule, the full actual H_m trajectory obeys

    P_par(H_m;r_0,0)
              < (2500/m^2) 2^(-2^m).              (15)

The same is true with the opposite initial horizontal sign, by symmetry.
This does not bound the family over other choices of r_0,v_0.

Here is a proof that tracks all later queried states, rather than inferring
the answer only from the first cancellation. Elementary parameter bounds are

    12/5<c<5/2, 2/5<a<3/7,
    2/5<sigma<3/7, 7/5<b0=1+a<10/7.

For the lower bound on a, use 1+sqrt(2)<17/7 and
(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon)>=127/129, giving a>889/2193>2/5.
The other inequalities follow by direct comparison of positive fractions.
Since c is greater than1+sqrt(2),

    0<sigma-a
      =epsilon[2lambda(lambda-1)+lambda^2 epsilon+c+1]
                                               /[c(c+1)]
      <2epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2).                 (16)

For the last bound the numerator in brackets is less than12, using
lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/128, while c(c+1)>204/25>8.

At the first actual step b0, (7) gives

    14/25<v_1=b0 sigma<30/49<5/8,
    r_1(1+v_1+r_1^2)=r_0(sigma-a),
    0<r_1<2/m.                                    (17)

We now prove that the entire remaining trajectory stays in the region

    1/2<=v<=3/4, |r|<=1/10.                        (18)

Inside this region the exact step rule gives explicit amplitude bounds.
For a level-zero step b0, the numerator factor
1+v+r^2-b0 is positive and at most1+3/4+1/100-7/5=9/25.
The denominator1+v_plus+r_plus^2 is at least3/2, so

    |r_plus| <= L0 |r|, L0=1/4.

For a level j>=1 step b_j=1+a c^j, one has b_j>=2-epsilon>1.76,
whereas1+v+r^2<=1.76. The numerator is therefore negative, and

    |r_plus| <= Lj |r|, Lj=(2/7)c^j.               (19)

Indeed its absolute value is less than a c^j and its denominator is at
least3/2, while a<3/7. These bounds use the current state in (18) but
do not require the next state to be in that region.

Let P_j be the product of these scalar bounds over the full word H_j,
with P_0=1, and put A0=2/7 and beta=c^2/14<1/2. For j>=1,

    P_j=beta^(2^(j-1))/(A0 c^(j+1)),
    theta_j=P_j Lj=beta^(2^(j-1))/c<1.             (20)

This follows either by multiplying the known counts of each ruler level
or by P_(j+1)=P_j^2 Lj and P_1=1/4. Every prefix product in every H_j
is at most1: it is true for H_0; in the right child the left-child and
central-step prefactor is P_j Lj=theta_j<1, and the induction applies
inside both children. At j=0 that prefactor is1/4.

For the accumulated vertical drift, let E_j be the weighted prefix sum

    E_j=sum_(i in H_j) h_i [product_(t<i)L_(level(t))]^2.

Then E_1=b0<3/2 and, for j>=1,

    E_(j+1)=(1+theta_j^2)E_j+P_j^2 b_j.            (21)

The two needed infinite bounds are elementary. Since c>2,beta<1/2,

    sum_(j>=1)theta_j^2
        <(1/4)sum_(j>=1)2^(-2^j)<=1/8.

Also b_j<c^j for j>=1: both a and c^(-j) are less than3/7. Therefore

    sum_(j>=1)P_j^2 b_j
       <sum_(j>=1)49*2^(-2j-5)=49/96.

For this estimate insert(20), use A0^(-2)=49/4, c>2,
beta^(2^j)<2^(-2^j), and2^j>=j+1. Finally
product(1+theta_j^2)<=1/[1-sum theta_j^2]<8/7,
as follows by expanding the finite products and bounding their elementary
symmetric sums by powers of the total sum. Passing to the limit preserves
the bound. Equations(21) consequently imply

    E_j<(3/2+49/96)(8/7)=193/84<3                 (22)

for every j.

In the suffix of H_m after its first step, every prefix product is at
most4, because reinserting that first L0 multiplies it by1/4. The suffix
weighted drift sum is at most16E_m<48. Hence, as long as(18) has held,

    |r_i|<=4r_1<8/m<=1/16<1/10,
    v_i<=v_1+48r_1^2<5/8+192/m^2<3/4.

The lower bound v_i>=v_1>1/2 follows from v_plus=v+h r^2. These strict
bounds prove(18) throughout by a first-exit argument: the same estimates
control a proposed first exiting step, which is impossible.

At the terminal sample the entire suffix product is4P_m. From(17),(20),

    r_n^2<(64/m^2)P_m^2.

The numerator of(8) is less than2r_n^2 by(18), and the initial denominator
sigma(1+sigma)^2 exceeds98/125>3/4. Thus

    P_par<3r_n^2<(192/m^2)P_m^2
          <(2352/m^2)2^(-2^m),

which proves(15). For the exact benchmark, accepted factb98bb807d514e80d
gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3), so this particular parabolic witness divided
by U_n^ZJ tends to zero. Only this fixed starting rule is excluded.

The responsible state variable is visible in(17): matching the geometric
ratio makes the critical first step almost annihilate the horizontal
coordinate. The subsequent normal state remains near its post-first-step
value, and the complete ruler-product/drift bounds above prevent recovery.
This is a statement about the entire actual legal trajectory, not a
one-pair dual test or a comparison with an independently reset right child.

## 7. The concrete remaining critical-word problem

For the actual critical word fix, within each horizon m,

    c_m=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a_m=(1-1/m)/c_m,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j).

Let T_h denote the explicit two-state transition (7). The full block map
is defined EXACTLY by

    Phi_0=identity,
    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j composed with T_(1+a_m c_m^j)
                                     composed with Phi_j.

The central task in this branch is to select explicit r_0(m),v_0(m), or
prove a useful uniform upper bound for this mechanism, while controlling
the exact payoff (8) under Phi_m. The model is already an actual legal
function for every such selection, so no separate interpolation optimization
is required. What is missing is the QUANTITATIVE estimate on that nonlinear
recursion. Neither (11), the small-amplitude calibration, nor (13) fills it.

An open subgoal is to compare the increase in v across both copies of a
dyadic block with the horizontal sign and gain at its central bridge.
The state v must remain shared between the copies; restarting it, freezing
the curvature, or replacing the two copies by independent instances would
change the function and lose the construction.

## 8. Source grounding and verification scope

Jiang, Yue and Zhou, arXiv:1911.12545v2 (2021), Theorem2.2, gives the
Euclidean projection onto S={(x,y):||x||^2<=y}: inside S it is the point,
and outside it is(x/(1+mu),y+mu/2), where mu>=max(0,-2y) is the unique
solution of ||x||^2/(1+mu)^2=y+mu/2. The paper's surrounding definition
of S and full proof were read. It supplies a closely matched projection
mechanism, not a GD complexity result for the present function. Its
cubic-regularization convergence theorem is not invoked.

Sections1--6 derive the two-dimensional specialization and all required
properties directly. No external theorem is used as a black box, and no
unverified earlier project result is a premise. The finite C59 data merely
motivated seeking dense turning gradients. Every new statement here remains
a draft until accepted by the verifier; no claim is made that this family
solves the all-schedule exact-profile problem.

Current allocation is first of TWO rounds after completed47, with ceilings49/50; no numerical/formal calls. Use original enclosing-m parameters beta=1-1/m,c=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m),a=beta/c, H2=(1+a,1+beta,1+a). On a face q+w, q perpendicular w, exact internal tangents are w,-a w,a beta w,-a^2 beta w. First derive the old-to-new remote normal needed by any two H2 face blocks with core resets; orthogonal normals appear impossible. Then realize the actual projection of the next point onto the sloping edge of an explicit triangle and follow the true gradient, instead of claiming the intended horizontal face. Balanced aspect makes that projection normal, allowing a closed full physical trajectory, true radius, all-Q verification and complete same-word/original-B comparison. All new arguments will be reproduced jointly as UNVERIFIED evidence; no fact_submit is authorized.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001156 -->
# C69: a joint chronological budget for quartic and horizon-dependent power epigraphs

Status: COMPLETE UNVERIFIED joint candidate, fifth launch round 1 (cumulative 12).
Sections 1--9 contain the full quartic proof; Section 10 derives the general
power extension. No component is imported as an unverified predecessor.
No fact_submit, trajectory evaluation, numerical optimization, installation,
or new allocation was used. The only accepted mathematical predecessor is
b98bb807d514e80d, used for the exact benchmark recursions. All function,
trajectory, block, and combinatorial assertions used below are proved here.
C65, C66, and C67 are context, not proof premises.

## 1. Definitions and result

In Sections 1--9 the power is FIXED at 4. For each integer m>=512 set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
    d_j=a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.

The H_j here are local schedule words, not the conjugate quadratics called
H_j in a different accepted fact. Every embedded word uses this SAME a,c.
Index physical steps by t=1,...,n. Let nu_2(t) be the exponent of 2 in t and
b(t) the number of 1 bits in the binary expansion of t, with b(0)=0. Then

    h_t=1+d_(nu_2(t)).                                     (1)

Define the single global function and its state coordinates by

    K_4={(r,s) in R^2:s>=r^4/4},
    F_4(X)=max_(g in K_4) (<X,g>-||g||^2/2),
    eta=v+r^4/4, u=eta r^2=vr^2+r^6/4,
    X(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v), G(r)=(r,r^4/4), v>=0.

Here r,v,u,eta and X,G denote local state variables. Write x for the
horizontal coordinate of X and R=|x|. Start from arbitrary r_0!=0 and
v_0>=0, and run the given H_m on this same F_4:

    X_t=X_(t-1)-h_t grad F_4(X_(t-1)), 1<=t<=n.

Section 2 proves global legality, the unique actual recurrence, and

    min F_4=0, argmin F_4={(0,y):y<=0},
    R_0=dist(X_0,argmin F_4)=|r_0|(1+u_0),
    2F_4(X(r,v))=r^2+(3/2)v r^4+(7/16)r^8.               (2)

A turn is a step t with x_(t-1)x_t<0. The following bounds hold jointly
for EVERY such initial state, without restricting the number or levels of
turns:

    R_t<=R_0 for every t,
    R_t<=c^(-b(t))R_0 at every turn t,                    (3)
    P_4:=2F_4(X_n)/R_0^2 <=3/(8S_m).                     (4)

The retained clock cost, including the nonnegative contributions at every
step, is

    J_all=(1/2)sum_(t=1)^n |r_(t-1)|^2(v_t-v_(t-1))
         =(1/8)sum_(t=1)^n h_t |r_(t-1)|^6.

It satisfies the explicit global charge

    J_all <= min{
       |r_0|^2(v_n-v_0)/2,
       R_0 |r_0|^5 [2(1+1/c)^m-1]/8
    },                                                   (5)

and, using only the original closest-minimizer distance,

    J_all <=4^(5/7) R_0^(12/7)[2(1+1/c)^m-1]/8.           (6)

No assertion that J_all vanishes, or that a logarithmic clock decreases
across zero, is made. The quantitative objective bound (4) uses the
chronological block budget directly; it does not delete J_all.

For the EXACT Zhang--Jiang denominator B_n defined in accepted fact
b98bb807d514e80d, Section 8 proves

    B_(2^m-1) P_4 <625/1536<1/2       for all m>=512,      (7)
    limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(r_0!=0,v_0>=0)
        B_(2^m-1) P_4 <=3lambda/(8e)<3/8.                (8)

This excludes the whole stated quartic epigraph family as a matching
adversary on these schedules. It is NOT an upper bound for unrestricted
W_n and does not settle the original all-schedule profile problem.

## 2. The global function, ordered pairs, distance, and physical recursion

K_4 is nonempty, closed, and convex. Completing the square in the defining
maximum shows that its unique maximizing g is the Euclidean projection
Pi_(K_4)(X). The normal inequalities at projections of X and Y give

    ||Pi_(K_4)(X)-Pi_(K_4)(Y)||^2
       <=<Pi_(K_4)(X)-Pi_(K_4)(Y),X-Y>.

Thus the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the defining maxima gives

    0<=F_4(Y)-F_4(X)-<Pi_(K_4)(X),Y-X>
      <=<Pi_(K_4)(Y)-Pi_(K_4)(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.

It follows that F_4 is differentiable with gradient Pi_(K_4), hence has
1-Lipschitz gradient. Convexity follows from its supremum of affine
functions representation.

For phi(r)=r^4/4, one has

    X(r,v)-G(r)=eta*(phi'(r),-1).

For any (z,s) in K_4 its inner product with (z,s)-G(r) is at most

    eta*[phi'(r)(z-r)-phi(z)+phi(r)]<=0.

The last inequality is the tangent inequality for the convex function
phi. This proves grad F_4(X(r,v))=G(r). Substitution into the maximum
proves the value in (2). In particular, if f_i=F_4(X_i), every ordered
pair of states in this construction has the exact residual

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>
        =eta_j*[r_j^3(r_i-r_j)-r_i^4/4+r_j^4/4]<=0.      (9)

There is no restriction on temporal separation in (9).

If x=0 and y<=0, the maximum is zero: zero is feasible, and every feasible
g=(r,s) has s>=0, so ys-||g||^2/2<=0. If x!=0, test g=(r,r^4/4)
with sufficiently small r of the sign of x; the positive term xr
dominates the terms of order 2 or higher. If x=0 and y>0, a sufficiently
small g=(0,s), s>0, gives positive value. This proves the exact minimizer
set in (2). Its closest point to (x,-v), v>=0, is (0,-v), proving the
true distance formula without retaining a spurious initial vertical norm.

The actual GD step of size h>=0 is exactly

    v_plus=v+h r^4/4,
    x_plus=x-hr,
    x_plus=T_(v_plus)(r_plus),
    T_v(r)=r+v r^3+r^7/4.                               (10)

T_v is odd and strictly increasing onto R, since

    T_v'(r)=1+3vr^2+7r^6/4>=1.

It therefore determines the unique actual r_plus. In particular
sign(r)=sign(x). If r reaches zero, x=0 and G(r)=0, so the trajectory is
absorbed with zero subsequent objective and clock cost.

On any interval where all states are nonzero and have the same sign,
R decreases and v increases. Equation (10) then shows that |r| decreases.
Reflection allows every such interval to be treated on the positive ray.

For later use, smoothness and the minimizer (0,-v) give 2F_4(X)<=R^2.
This can also be checked from the explicit state formula: with z=r^6/4,

    2F_4(X)/r^2=1+(3/2)u+z/4,
    R^2/r^2=(1+u)^2,

and their difference is u/2+u^2-z/4>=u/4+u^2>=0 because 0<=z<=u.

## 3. A no-turn block pays for the loss of normal state

Consider any actual occurrence of H_l, l>=1, along which every state is
nonzero and has the same sign. No entry v is reset. Let r>0 and u>0 be
the terminal gradient magnitude and effective normal state, and let
rho>0 and u_mid be their counterparts immediately BEFORE the middle
step 1+d_(l-1). Since that step does not turn or annihilate,

    u_mid>d_(l-1).                                      (11)

Monotonicity on this interval gives rho>=r and v_mid<=v_end. Consequently

    u_mid=v_mid rho^2+rho^6/4
          <=(rho/r)^6 (v_end r^2+r^6/4)
          =(rho/r)^6 u.                                (12)

Let Apre_l=S_(l-1)+1+d_(l-1)=M_(l-1)+d_(l-1) be the sum of steps
through the middle step. Every gradient used by those steps has
magnitude at least rho; every later gradient has magnitude at least r.
Telescoping the actual horizontal motion, with R_entry its input radius,
therefore gives

    R_entry/r
      >=M_l+u+Apre_l*((d_(l-1)/u)^(1/6)-1)_+.            (13)

Here (z)_+=max(z,0). If u>=d_(l-1)/2, the explicit u in (13) is at
least d_(l-1)/2. If u<d_(l-1)/2, then

    (d_(l-1)/u)^(1/6)>2^(1/6)>11/10,

because (11/10)^6=1771561/1000000<2. Since Apre_l>=d_(l-1), the
last term in (13) exceeds d_(l-1)/10. Thus in both cases

    R_entry >=Aend_l |r_end|,
    Aend_l:=M_l+d_(l-1)/10.                             (14)

For the empty word define Aend_0=1; the same gradient bound is the state
identity R=|r|(1+u)>=|r|. If an endpoint is absorbed, the gradient bound
with its zero terminal gradient holds as well.

This is the key replacement for a frozen-normal argument. A decrease in
terminal u is permitted. Equation (12) charges that decrease to earlier
gradient magnitudes, and equation (13) charges those magnitudes to the
TRUE horizontal entry distance. No inequality u_end>=u_mid is used.

## 4. Uniform capacity of every no-turn H_l in the enclosing word

The word recursion implies M_(l+1)=2M_l+d_l and M_0=1. Set

    A_mass=a/(c-2).

Solving this elementary recurrence gives

    M_l=A_mass c^l+(1-A_mass)2^l.                        (15)

Since lambda(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda, one has c(c-2)>1>1-epsilon.
It follows that 0<A_mass<1. In particular,

    M_l/d_l>=1/(c-2).                                  (16)

For m>=512, sqrt(2)<99/70 gives

    c<(169/70)(513/512)<121/50.

The function z-1/(z-2)-1/(10z) is strictly increasing for z>2. At
z=121/50 its value is -289/127050<0. Therefore

    1/(c-2)+1/(10c)>c.

Combining this with (14) and (16) proves

    Aend_l>=c d_l=a c^(l+1)        for l>=1.             (17)

For l=0 the same conclusion holds because

    Aend_0=1>ac=1-1/m.                                  (18)

Equations (17)--(18) are uniform over the block position, initial state,
and l. They use the enclosing a,c throughout.

## 5. A binary interval lemma and chronological turn accounting

We first prove the combinatorial statement required to combine real
one-sign intervals. If integers 0<=s<t satisfy

    q=b(t-1)-b(s)>=1,

then [s,t] contains an aligned closed dyadic interval [A,A+2^q], where
A is a multiple of 2^q.

Proof. Put B=2^q, write s=kB+r with 0<=r<B, and suppose no such
interval is contained. If r=0, the first possible interval starts at s,
so t<s+B. Then t-1 has high binary part k and a low part at most B-2;
hence b(t-1)-b(s)<=q-1, a contradiction.

If r>0, the first possible start is (k+1)B, so t<(k+2)B. If t-1
has high part k, its low part has at most q ones, whereas b(r)>=1,
again giving difference at most q-1. If t-1 has high part k+1, its
low part is at most B-2 and has at most q-1 ones. Adding 1 to k
increases its bit count by at most 1. Thus the difference is at most
1+(q-1)-b(r)<=q-1. This contradiction proves the lemma.             (19)

An aligned interval [A,A+2^q] corresponds to the actual subword H_q
at physical step indices A+1,...,A+2^q-1: for 0<j<2^q,
nu_2(A+j)=nu_2(j). This uses the same physical a,c.

Now let s<t be consecutive actual turn times, allowing s=0 for the
initial boundary before the first turn. The steps s+1,...,t-1 form
one same-sign interval. Set

    q=max{0,b(t-1)-b(s)}, ell=nu_2(t).

If q>=1, (19) finds an actual no-turn H_q inside this interval. Its
entry horizontal radius is at most R_s, and its terminal gradient
magnitude is at least |r_(t-1)|, by monotonicity on the entire interval.
Equations (14) and (17) therefore give

    R_s>=Aend_q |r_(t-1)|>=a c^(q+1)|r_(t-1)|.           (20)

For q=0, use R_s>=|r_(t-1)| and (18) to get the same result. At the
actual turn t, equation (10) gives

    R_t=(d_ell-u_(t-1))|r_(t-1)|
        <=a c^ell |r_(t-1)|
        <=c^(ell-q-1)R_s.                              (21)

The elementary binary identity

    b(t-1)=b(t)-1+nu_2(t)

and q>=b(t-1)-b(s) imply

    R_t<=c^(b(s)-b(t))R_s.                              (22)

Starting at s=0 and telescoping (22) over the ACTUAL turns proves the
second part of (3). Between turns the radius decreases, proving the
first part of (3). This argument allows arbitrary repeated rises and
falls in turn level. It neither optimizes right copies independently
nor assumes that u is monotone.

## 6. Global payment of the retained moving-normal cost

For context, on a positive one-sign interval define the local clock

    C_4(r,v)=log r+(3/2)vr^2+(7/24)r^6,
    Psi(x,v)=C_4(T_v^(-1)(x),v), x>0.

Differentiating T_v(r)=x gives

    partial_x Psi=1/r, partial_v Psi=r^2/2.

For one positive noncrossing step, first move horizontally at the old v
and then increase v at the new horizontal coordinate. Monotonicity of
T_v^(-1) bounds the two resulting integrals and yields

    C_4(r_plus,v_plus)-C_4(r,v)
       <=-h+(r^2/2)(v_plus-v).                          (23)

Reflection covers a negative one-sign interval. This derivation retains
the positive cost, and does not extend the logarithm across annihilation
or sign changes.

The global radius result (3), together with v_t>=v_0 and monotonicity of
T_v on the positive ray, implies

    |r_t|<=|r_0|  for every t.                           (24)

The first bound in (5) now follows by summing the defining weighted
vertical increments of J_all.

To obtain the second bound, set TV_x=sum_(t=1)^n h_t|r_(t-1)|, the
total absolute horizontal displacement. At a nonturn step (including
one that reaches zero), its contribution is R_(t-1)-R_t. At a turn it
is R_(t-1)+R_t. Consequently the exact telescoping identity is

    TV_x=R_0-R_n+2 sum_(t a turn) R_t.                   (25)

Use (3), and expand the product over the m independent binary digits:

    sum_(t=0)^(2^m-1)c^(-b(t))=(1+1/c)^m.

It follows that

    TV_x<=R_0[2(1+1/c)^m-1].                            (26)

Since J_all=(1/8)sum h_t|r_(t-1)|*|r_(t-1)|^5, (24)--(26) prove
the second bound in (5). Finally R_0>=|r_0|^7/4 by the state formula,
so R_0|r_0|^5<=4^(5/7)R_0^(12/7), proving (6).

Thus the sum of all one-sign J costs is bounded by (5)--(6), since it
is no larger than J_all. The bounds retain the initial radius and,
in the first version, the actual terminal vertical state. They remain
valid for trajectories that annihilate; their subsequent contributions
are zero. No lower bound on nonzero |r_t| is needed.

## 7. Terminal payoff, with every entry-radius factor retained

Consider any nonzero same-sign interval with total step mass T>=1/6.
Write r>0 for its terminal gradient magnitude and u=vr^2+r^6/4 for
its terminal normal state. The actual recursion, without resetting v,
gives

    R_entry>=r(T+1+u),
    v_end>=T r^4/4.

Put z=r^6/4. Then z<=u/(T+1), and (2) implies

    2F_4(X_end)/R_entry^2
       <=[1+k_T u]/(T+1+u)^2,
    k_T=3/2+1/[4(T+1)].

The derivative of the last rational function has the sign of
k_T(T+1)-2-k_T u. Since T>=1/6, its maximum on u>=0 is attained at
u=T+1-2/k_T and equals

    E_4(T)=(6T+7)^2/[48(T+1)^2(2T+1)]<=3/(8T).          (27)

For the last comparison the numerator of the difference, over denominator
48T(T+1)^2(2T+1), is 6T^2+23T+18>0. An absorbing terminal state has
zero payoff and satisfies the desired bound separately.

If the full run is nonabsorbed, let s be its last actual turn, or let
s=0 if it has no turns. Set b=b(s) and q=m-b. Apply (19) with
t=2^m. The final same-sign interval contains an actual H_q. If q>=1,
its full mass T is at least S_q>=S_1>1, so (3) and (27) give

    P_4<=c^(-2b)*3/(8S_q).                              (28)

The factor c^(-2b) in (28) is the ACTUAL entry-radius ratio bounded in
terms of the original R_0. A later block has not been renormalized as a
fresh problem.

For j>=1, (15) gives

    S_(j+1)-c S_j=c-1-(c-2)(1-A_mass)2^j<=c-1.

Since S_j>1 and c>1, this implies

    S_(j+1)<(2c-1)S_j<c^2 S_j.

Iterating yields S_m<=c^(2b)S_q, so (28) is at most 3/(8S_m).

If q=0, then b(s)=m and s=n, so the last step turns. Use the terminal
state bound 2F_4<=R_n^2 and (3) to obtain P_4<=c^(-2m). Since (15)
gives S_m<c^m and c^m>8/3 for m>=512, this is smaller than 3/(8S_m).
This proves (4) for every initial state, including all turn patterns.

## 8. The exact benchmark and the uniform comparison

Accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d defines the exact primitive and dominant
benchmark recursions. In their mass coordinates C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and
B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 they are

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

We prove directly from these recursions that

    B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).                             (29)

Set alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda) and
Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Both coefficients in Llin exceed 1, so
Llin>=x+y. Direct expansion, using lambda^2=2lambda+1, gives

    alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]
      =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.

Thus Llin>=Omega(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy. At
z=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial with larger root G(x,lambda y) is

    z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2
      =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.

Also z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so z is to the right of that
polynomial's vertex. Hence G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y).
Induction in the exact recursions proves B_n<=lambda C_n.

Put gamma=log_2(lambda)>1, D(t)=(1+t)^gamma-1, and
ell(z)=log D(exp z). For t=exp z, differentiation gives

    ell''(z)=gamma*t*(1+t)^(gamma-2)
      *[(1+t)^gamma-1-gamma*t]/D(t)^2>0.

The last bracket has value zero at t=0 and positive derivative for t>0.
Convexity therefore implies D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 set
Z=(x^(1/gamma)+y^(1/gamma))^gamma. Taking t=(y/x)^(1/gamma) shows
(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy. Thus Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0; also Z>=x+y, by convexity
of the positive power. Consequently Omega(x,y)<=Z. The zero cases
follow by direct substitution. Induction yields C_n<=(n+1)^gamma,
which, with B_n<=lambda C_n, proves (29).

For a finite comparison, c<121/50 and m>=512 imply

    A_mass=(1-1/m)/[c(c-2)]
      >(511/512)/(2541/2500)>49/50.

Since c>2 and m>=512, c^(-m)<1/50. Equation (15) therefore gives

    S_m/c^m>24/25.

The first three binomial terms show

    (1+1/m)^m>=2+(m-1)/(2m)>=12/5,

where the final inequality already holds for m>=5. Combining (4),
(29), lambda<5/2, and (lambda/c)^m<=5/12 gives

    B_n P_4
       <(3/8)*(5/2)*(5/12)*(25/24)
       =625/1536<1/2,

proving (7).

As m tends to infinity, A_mass tends to 1, c tends to lambda, and

    S_m/c^m=A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m-c^(-m) ->1,
    (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m) ->1/e.

Every bound above is uniform over r_0!=0,v_0>=0, so (4) and (29)
give (8). Finally e>1+1+1/2=5/2>lambda, proving 3lambda/(8e)<3/8.
No limiting formula for the exact sequence B_n has been assumed.

## 9. Scope, literature, and symbolic audit

The proof addresses the whole unscaled quartic epigraph model stated in
Section 1, at all initial horizontal radii and nonnegative vertical
states, including horizon-dependent choices. Its global charge and
payoff combine arbitrary actual turn histories. Lower-level reactivation
is allowed. This is a proper-family exclusion, not a conclusion about
all functions, other powers, or the original all-h target.

The projection background is consistent with Propositions 3.2--3.3 of
Chierchia, Pustelnik, Pesquet, and Pesquet-Popescu, "Epigraphical splitting
for solving constrained convex formulations of inverse problems with
proximal tools", arXiv:1210.5844v3. Their Hilbert product is Euclidean;
their scalar generator has full domain and is proper closed convex;
beta=4, tau=1/4, zeta=-v yield T_v(r)=x. The exact locally downloaded
paper's definitions and projection proof were read. No external result
from it is used as a proof premise. The dedicated theorem searches for
the moving-state budget and the binary interval lemma returned no usable
result; both have been proved directly here.

Symbolic boundary checks:

* Consecutive actual turns have an empty intervening H_0. The necessary
  charge is exactly Aend_0>=ac; no unqueried lower-level step is invented.
* The aligned case [s,t]=[A,A+2^q] uses the full actual H_q, with no
  missing first or last step and no change of enclosing parameters.
* Negative binary-count differences are handled by q=max(0,difference)
  in (20)--(22), not by a fictitious word of negative length.
* A turn at the final step has q=0 and uses the smoothness bound, not
  formula (27) at zero mass.
* Exact zero is absorbing. No logarithm is evaluated there, and the
  main payoff proof uses no logarithm at all.
* Very small nonzero gradients cause no reciprocal estimate with an
  uncontrolled constant; (13) charges small terminal u explicitly.
* The proof does not freeze v, assume monotonicity of u, or discard the
  positive moving-normal cost. The complete all-pair function and true
  denominator were established before the block argument.

The separate possible concave-clock integral explored in rough local
notes is unnecessary here and is not a premise. No verification success
is asserted until an authorized fact_submit actually accepts this joint
statement and proof.

## 10. Joint extension to horizon-dependent powers

This section extends the result to every real power satisfying

    2<p<=m/256, with m an integer and m>=512.             (30)

The power p may depend on m, but is FIXED during the entire trajectory.
All a,c,d_j,H_j,S_j,M_j retain their enclosing-m definitions in Section 1.
The sufficient range (30) is not claimed to be necessary. For p=4,
Sections 1--9 already give the stronger threshold m>=512.

The resulting complete joint claim is

    P_p:=2F_p(X_n)/R_0^2<=1/(2S_m),                      (31)
    B_n P_p<125/256<1/2,                                (32)
    limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(2<p<=m/256,r_0!=0,v_0>=0)
        B_n P_p<=lambda/(2e)<1/2.                       (33)

In particular every fixed real p>2 is eventually covered. The supremum
in (33) permits horizon-dependent initial states and powers throughout
the displayed range. Powers outside that range remain unaddressed by
this sufficient argument. Sections 10.1--10.5 derive all additional
function and trajectory formulas; the elementary binary interval and
benchmark proofs already included in Sections 5 and 8 are part of this
same joint proof, not unverified external premises.

### 10.1. The general power function and exact inherited state

For real p>2 define

    phi_p(r)=|r|^p/p,
    K_p={(r,s):s>=phi_p(r)},
    F_p(X)=max_(g in K_p)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2),
    eta=v+phi_p(r), u=eta |r|^(p-2),
    X_p(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v), G_p(r)=(r,phi_p(r)), v>=0.

The epigraph K_p is nonempty, closed, and convex. Completing the square
makes the maximizing gradient its unique Euclidean projection.
The two projection normal inequalities give

    ||Pi_(K_p)(X)-Pi_(K_p)(Y)||^2
       <=<Pi_(K_p)(X)-Pi_(K_p)(Y),X-Y>.

Comparing the defining maxima bounds the first-order remainder between
zero and ||X-Y||^2, as in the explicit derivation in Section 2. Hence
F_p is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable, and has 1-Lipschitz
gradient equal to the projection.

Since phi_p'(r)=r|r|^(p-2), one has

    X_p-G_p=eta*(phi_p'(r),-1).

Its inner product with any feasible displacement (z,s)-G_p is at most
eta*[phi_p'(r)(z-r)-phi_p(z)+phi_p(r)]<=0. This proves the exact
gradient formula at every state, and substitution gives

    2F_p(X_p(r,v))
      =r^2+(2-2/p)v|r|^p+(2p-1)|r|^(2p)/p^2.            (34)

For arbitrary two such states, their ordered interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=eta_j*[phi_p'(r_j)(r_i-r_j)-phi_p(r_i)
                                  +phi_p(r_j)]<=0.       (35)

This follows by expanding the residual to
<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>, so includes every ordered pair, not merely neighbors.

Zero is feasible in the maximum. At (0,y), y<=0, every feasible
inner product minus the squared norm is nonpositive. At x!=0, choose a
sufficiently small r of the sign of x; the positive linear term xr
dominates terms of order 2,p,2p. At x=0,y>0 a small positive vertical
gradient gives positive value. Therefore

    min F_p=0, argmin F_p={(0,y):y<=0},
    R_0=dist(X_p(r_0,v_0),argmin F_p)=|r_0|(1+u_0).       (36)

The actual transition is

    v_plus=v+h|r|^p/p, x_plus=x-hr,
    x_plus=T_(p,v_plus)(r_plus),
    T_(p,v)(r)=r+v r|r|^(p-2)+r|r|^(2p-2)/p.            (37)

The last map is odd, continuous, strictly increasing onto R; off zero
its derivative is
1+(p-1)v|r|^(p-2)+(2p-1)|r|^(2p-2)/p>=1, and its derivative at
zero is 1. Thus the next state is uniquely that of the same F_p.

On a same-sign nonzero interval, R and |r| decrease while v increases.
At zero the state is absorbing. Put z=|r|^(2p-2)/p. Then

    2F_p(X)/r^2=1+(2-2/p)u+z/p,
    0<=z<=u.

It follows that 2F_p(X)<=R^2, since the difference after division by
r^2 is (2/p)u+u^2-z/p>=u/p+u^2>=0.

### 10.2. General-power endpoint capacity with every p factor explicit

Set

    delta_p=2^(1/(2p-2))-1.

Since p>2, one has 0<delta_p<sqrt(2)-1<1/2. Consider a nonzero
one-sign actual H_l, l>=1. Use r,u for its terminal magnitude and normal
state, and rho,u_mid immediately before its middle step. Equation (37)
and preservation of sign imply u_mid>d_(l-1). Monotonicity gives

    u_mid
       <=(rho/r)^(2p-2) u.

Indeed the v term has exponent p-2 and v_mid<=v_end, while rho/r>=1;
the pure-power term has exponent 2p-2. Summing horizontal displacements
through the middle step and the rest of the same actual block yields

    R_entry/r>=M_l+u
       +Apre_l*((d_(l-1)/u)^(1/(2p-2))-1)_+,
    Apre_l=M_(l-1)+d_(l-1)>=d_(l-1).                    (38)

If u>=d_(l-1)/2, its contribution alone is at least
delta_p d_(l-1), because delta_p<1/2. Otherwise the last factor in
(38) is greater than delta_p. Thus

    R_entry>=Aend_(p,l)|r_end|,
    Aend_(p,l)=M_l+delta_p d_(l-1) for l>=1,
    Aend_(p,0)=1.                                      (39)

Here the subscript p distinguishes this coefficient from the quartic
rational lower bound in Section 3.

We now prove the capacity needed for all actual turn gaps:

    Aend_(p,l)>=c d_l for every l>=0.                   (40)

For l=0, use ac=1-1/m<1. For l>=1, equation (15), derived from the
word recurrence and independent of p, gives

    Aend_(p,l)/d_l>=1/(c-2)+delta_p/c.

Thus it suffices to prove

    delta_p>= [c/(c-2)]*[c(c-2)-1].                     (41)

The constants in (41) are controlled explicitly. Since
lambda>12/5 and z/(z-2) decreases for z>2,

    c/(c-2)<6.

Also

    c(c-2)-1
      =2lambda(lambda-1)/m+lambda^2/m^2
      <(15/2)/m+(25/4)/m^2<8/m,

where the last inequality uses m>=512. The right side of (41) is
therefore less than 48/m. On the other hand log(2)>1/2, by integrating
1/t>1/2 on (1,2), and exp(z)-1>z for z>0. Hence

    delta_p>1/[4(p-1)]>1/(4p)>=64/m

under (30). This proves (41) and (40). No p-dependent loss is hidden
inside an asymptotic term.

### 10.3. Combining all sign changes and paying the full cost

The binary interval lemma (19) was proved for integers alone. For
consecutive actual turns s<t, with s=0 allowed initially, set

    q=max{0,b(t-1)-b(s)}, ell=nu_2(t).

If q>=1, that lemma locates a real, aligned, nonturn H_q inside
steps s+1,...,t-1. Its input radius is at most R_s and its endpoint
gradient magnitude is at least |r_(t-1)|. Formula (40) gives

    R_s>=a c^(q+1)|r_(t-1)|.

If q=0, this follows from R_s>=|r_(t-1)| and ac<1. At turn t,
the exact recurrence (37) gives

    R_t=(d_ell-u_(t-1))|r_(t-1)|
       <=c^(ell-q-1)R_s
       <=c^(b(s)-b(t))R_s.

The last inequality uses b(t-1)=b(t)-1+ell. Induction over the ACTUAL
turns, followed by monotonicity between them, proves

    R_t<=c^(-b(t))R_0 at every turn,
    R_t<=R_0, |r_t|<=|r_0| at every state.               (42)

The last assertion follows from v_t>=v_0 and monotonicity of the inverse
in (37). Nothing in this proof restricts the chronology of turn levels.

For the original power clock on a single positive interval,

    C_p(r,v)=log r+(p-1)v r^(p-2)/(p-2)
                         +(2p-1)r^(2p-2)/[p(2p-2)],

implicit differentiation at fixed horizontal x gives the positive
vertical derivative r^(p-2)/(p-2). Splitting a noncrossing step into
horizontal motion and then its actual vertical increment yields

    C_p(r_plus,v_plus)-C_p(r,v)
       <=-h+|r|^(p-2)(v_plus-v)/(p-2).

Accordingly define the full, nonnegative retained cost by

    J_(p,all)
      =sum |r_(t-1)|^(p-2)(v_t-v_(t-1))/(p-2)
      =sum h_t |r_(t-1)|^(2p-2)/[p(p-2)].

The exact total-variation identity is still
TV_x=R_0-R_n+2 sum_(t a turn) R_t. Expanding over all m binary digits
and using (42) gives TV_x<=R_0[2(1+1/c)^m-1]. Therefore

    J_(p,all)<=min{
      |r_0|^(p-2)(v_n-v_0)/(p-2),
      R_0|r_0|^(2p-3)[2(1+1/c)^m-1]/[p(p-2)]
    }.                                                  (43)

Since R_0>=|r_0|^(2p-1)/p, one also has the radius-only charge

    J_(p,all)
       <=p^(-2/(2p-1)) R_0^((4p-4)/(2p-1))
                         [2(1+1/c)^m-1]/(p-2).           (44)

The factor 1/(p-2) is explicit in every version. The charge (44) can
diverge as p approaches 2; no contrary uniform bound is asserted.
The objective proof below does not absorb this factor into o(1).
Absorbed trajectories have zero subsequent cost, and no logarithm is
taken across a zero or a sign change.

### 10.4. Uniform no-turn payoff and the full-word conclusion

On a same-sign nonzero interval of mass T, the exact recurrence gives

    R_entry>=r(T+1+u),
    v_end>=T r^p/p,
    z=r^(2p-2)/p<=u/(T+1).

Combining this with (34), and maximizing the resulting rational
function of u>=0, gives for T>=1/(2p-2)

    2F_p(X_end)/R_entry^2<=E_p(T),
    E_p(T)=[(2p-2)T+2p-1]^2
              /[4p(p-1)(T+1)^2(2T+1)].                  (45)

For completeness, the rational function is
[1+k_T u]/[T+1+u]^2 with
k_T=2-2/p+1/[p(T+1)]. Its derivative has the sign of
k_T(T+1)-2-k_T u; the displayed condition makes its maximizing
u=T+1-2/k_T nonnegative and substitution yields (45).

There is a bound independent of p>2:

    E_p(T)<=1/(2T).                                    (46)

To check it, put A=p-1>1 and clear the positive denominator
4pA T(T+1)^2(2T+1). The numerator of the difference between the
right and left sides of (46) is

    4A T^3+(2A^2+6A)T^2+(4A^2+4A-1)T+2A(A+1)>0.

If s is the last turn, or s=0 for no turns, put b=b(s), q=m-b.
When q>=1, the final interval contains a real H_q by (19). Its mass
T>=S_q>1 is above the threshold in (45), since p>2. Retaining the
original input radius via (42) gives

    P_p<=c^(-2b)/(2S_q)<=1/(2S_m).

The second inequality follows from S_(j+1)<c^2 S_j for j>=1,
proved directly from the word recurrence in Section 7. If q=0, the
last step turns and P_p<=c^(-2m)<1/(2S_m), since S_m<c^m and
c^m>2. Absorption gives P_p=0. This proves (31) for all initial
states and every allowed p, without excluding reactivation.

### 10.5. Exact benchmark, uniformity, and the remaining power range

Section 8 proved directly from the accepted exact benchmark recursions
that B_n<=lambda^(m+1), and that S_m/c^m>24/25 for m>=512.
For a slightly sharper finite comparison than used there, the first
five binomial terms give

    (1+1/m)^m
      >2+49/100+4/25+1/25=269/100>8/3.

Indeed all three factors 1-j/m, j=1,2,3, exceed 99/100 for
m>=512. The second-, third-, and fourth-order binomial terms then
exceed, respectively, 49/100, (99/100)^2/6>4/25, and
(99/100)^3/24>1/25. Consequently (lambda/c)^m<3/8, and (31)
implies

    B_n P_p
       <(1/2)*(5/2)*(3/8)*(25/24)
       =125/256<1/2.

This proves (32). The limiting comparison uses only quantities
independent of p:

    S_m/c^m->1, (lambda/c)^m->1/e.

Thus (31) gives (33) uniformly over the entire range (30), with
no p-dependent factor inside its limiting argument. Since e>5/2>lambda,
the limiting constant lambda/(2e) is strictly below 1/2.

This complete joint extension still excludes only the stated global
power-epigraph functions. It does not upper-bound W_n or decide the
original exact-profile conjecture. It leaves p>m/256 outside its stated
sufficient range. A failed application of (41) in that range would
only be a failure of this capacity estimate, not a proof that the
power model succeeds or fails there.

All statements remain UNVERIFIED. The prior C68 shared finding is not
a predecessor; its component proofs have been reproduced inside this
joint artifact, and every additional general-power ingredient was
derived explicitly above. No fact_submit, experiment, or new allocation
has been inferred from this extension.

# Exact global three-step primitive capacity, including arbitrary terminal credit

Status: complete self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No
solver or fact_submit was used. The two polynomial identities and their
explicit coefficient sign proofs are included in full; no numerical
calculation or computational artifact is a proof premise.

The public max4 candidates 5b4359baa88bf3e4 and 500d209094028aef suggested
the mixed witness. Every ingredient used here is proved explicitly;
those unverified findings and the earlier max3 C32 are not premises.
The sole accepted predecessor is smooth-convex interpolation fact
1252b8823bb5a290.

## 1. Theorem and scope

For any nonnegative three-step schedule h=(u,v,w), put T=1+u+v+w.
For a legal convex differentiable 1-smooth GD run define

    V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)
        +sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
        -(T-1)T||g_3||^2.

Call h primitive if V_h>=0 for every such finite-dimensional run with
a minimizer. Put t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t and T_0=lambda^2=3+2t.

Then every primitive h satisfies

    T<=T_0.

Equality holds only for h=(t,2,t), and an explicit universally valid
interpolation certificate below proves attainment.

More strongly, if T>=T_0 and h!=(t,2,t), there is a legal finite-dimensional
run with V_h<0 and ALL Q_(i,3)=0, i=0,1,2. Consequently replacing V_h
by V_h minus ANY real linear combination of those three incoming-terminal
interpolation residuals cannot permit a larger capacity, or another
equality schedule. This is a necessity statement for arbitrary terminal
credit, not a sufficiency assertion for negative credit at Silver.

This proves the exact capacity at length three only. It does not bound
all-length primitive capacities and does not resolve the unrestricted
asymptotic normalized-objective target.

Sections2--4 first reduce any possible exception to

    t<u<7/4, 1<v<2, 1<w<t, T_0<=T<7,
    u+w>=2T/lambda-2.                              (R)

Section5 eliminates this entire remaining region.

## 2. Actual witnesses and all their legality conditions

For any legal run use

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0,
    V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)
         +sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
         -(T-1)T||g_3||^2.

For data with f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and x_3=g_3, set
y_i=x_i-g_i. Expansion gives

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_(i,3)=0,
    V_h=||x_0||^2-T^2||g_3||^2.                   (2)

All witnesses used below have these properties and include the legal
optimum jet (0,0,0). Besides the finite Q conditions, optimum legality
requires <g_i,y_i>>=0; the other direction is saturated by the value
formula. A feasible finite array is realized by the conjugate maximum
of quadratics in accepted 1252b8823bb5a290. Thus the arrays specify
fixed globally smooth convex functions with a minimizer.

The interval-projection witness at any selected step b, with ordinary
sums P,Q, has gradient 1 before b and -(b-1)/(Q+1) after it, starting
at P+1 and ending at its negative threshold, when b>1. Formula (2)
gives the necessary cut condition

    T(b-1)_+<=(P+1)(Q+1).                         (3)

This is a direct fixed clipped-derivative construction, including zero
ordinary steps. Apply it at the first and last step to obtain

    u,w<=M(T):=2T/(T+1)<2.                        (4)

For u,v,w>1 abbreviate

    a=u-1, A=u+1, r=v-1, D=w+1, eta=r/D;
    T=A+D+r.

The full-curvature quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, gives

    F_Q=1-T^2 a^2 r^2(w-1)^2>=0.                  (5)

The middle-cut interval, with x=(A,1,-r,-eta) and
g=(1,1,-eta,-eta), gives

    F_B=A^2-T^2 eta^2>=0, or Tr<=AD.              (6)

If r(w-1)<=1, the one-sided Huber derivative min(x,1), started at A,
has x=(A,1,-r,r(w-1)) and g=(1,1,-r,r(w-1)). It gives

    F_C=A^2-T^2 r^2(w-1)^2>=0.                   (7)

When eta a<1, a two-dimensional mixed witness gives

    F_N=a u F_B+(1+eta)u^2(2-u^2)>=0.             (8)

Here is its complete verification. Combine the middle-cut base array
from (6) with the auxiliary scalar array

    x_aux=(1,-a,ra,eta a),
    g_aux=(1,-a,eta a,eta a),
    f_aux,i=x_aux,i*g_aux,i-g_aux,i^2/2.

The auxiliary proximal points are (0,0,w eta a,0). Its only positive
finite residual is Q_aux,(0,2)=(1-eta a)w eta a. Every optimum
inequality is legal because g_aux,i*y_aux,i>=0. The base proximal
points are (u,0,-w eta,0); its Q_(0,2)=-(1+eta)w eta and all its
residuals are nonpositive. With

    epsilon=(1+eta)/[a(1-eta a)],

take the orthogonal sum of the base and sqrt(epsilon) times the
auxiliary points and gradients, with the summed values. Residuals add;
the sole positive auxiliary edge is exactly canceled and every other
edge is nonpositive. The energy equals

    F_B+epsilon(1-T^2 eta^2 a^2)
       =F_N/[a(1-eta a)].                         (9)

For the algebra use 1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2). This proves (8) with
all actual edges, rather than an assumed two-dimensional interpolant.

## 3. A uniform box where all four witnesses apply

Assume T>=T_0 and all the cut conditions (3). First v>1. Otherwise
T=1+u+v+w<=2+2M(T), so T^2-5T-2<=0. At T=T_0 this polynomial
equals 2t>0 and it increases thereafter, a contradiction.

Thus r>0 and the middle cut (6) applies. As T=A+D+r,

    T(T-A-D)<=AD<=(A+D)^2/4,
    T<=lambda(A+D)/2<=lambda(M(T)+1).

Consequently

    T^2+(1-3lambda)T-lambda<=0.                    (10)

Its left side increases for T>=T_0, and at T=7 equals
56-22lambda>0 because lambda<5/2. Hence T<7 and (4) gives
u,w<7/4.

Both u and w exceed1. If, for example, u<=1, then A<=2, D<11/4,
so r=T-A-D>T-19/4. But

    Tr>T(T-19/4)>=T_0(T_0-19/4)
       =11/4+5t/2>11/2>AD,

contradicting the middle cut. The argument for w is identical.

On this entire necessary box the extra hypotheses of (7) and (8) hold:

    r(w-1)<=A(w^2-1)/T<363/(64T_0)<1,
    eta a<=Aa/T=(u^2-1)/T<33/(16T_0)<1.           (11)

For the first strict numerical comparison, 363<64T_0 is equivalent
to 171<128sqrt(2), and squaring gives 29241<32768. Thus no local
neighborhood restriction is being silently used in this global screen.
The earlier inequality T<=lambda(A+D)/2 also gives

    u+w>=2T/lambda-2>=2t.                         (12)

## 4. Excluding three of the four endpoint orientations

Suppose u<=t. By (12), w>=t. Since T>=T_0 and w<2, the difference
between the two sides of the necessary inequality Tr(w-1)<=A is
at least

    T_0(T_0-t-w-2)(w-1)-(t+1)
       =T_0(w-t)(2-w)>=0.                         (13)

It is strictly positive unless u=w=t and T=T_0. In that exceptional
case v=2. Hence every other candidate must have u>t.

Suppose next that also w>=t. On the rectangle [t,M(T)]^2 consider

    q_T(u,w)=T(u-1)(w-1)(T-u-w-2).

This function is strictly concave in either endpoint with the other
fixed. Its minimum is attained at a corner. At T=T_0, M(T_0)=1+t/2,
the corner values are

    q_(T_0)(t,t)=1,
    q_(T_0)(t,M(T_0))=q_(T_0)(M(T_0),t)=lambda/2>1,
    q_(T_0)(M(T_0),M(T_0))=lambda/2>1.

Each corner value strictly increases with T>=T_0. All its positive
factors increase; in particular the derivatives of
T-t-M(T)-2 and T-2M(T)-2 are respectively
1-2/(T+1)^2 and 1-4/(T+1)^2, both positive.
Thus q_T>1 except at the single point T=T_0,u=w=t. This contradicts
the quadratic condition (5). Every nonexceptional candidate therefore
satisfies u>t>w.

Finally its middle step is less than2. For u>t the expression F_N in
(8), with u,w held fixed, strictly decreases as r>0 increases: the
negative squared term in F_B decreases, while u^2(2-u^2)<0.
At r=1 a direct expansion gives

    D^2 F_N/u
       =P_u(D):=u^2 D^2+(4-2u^2-u^3)D-(u-1)(u+2)^2.

This polynomial has one positive and one negative root, since its
leading coefficient is positive and its constant term negative. Also

    P_u(lambda)=-(lambda+1)u^3-2u^2+4(lambda+1)<0

for u>t: the right side vanishes at t and has strictly negative
derivative for u>0. Its positive root is therefore beyond lambda,
and P_u(D)<0 for 0<D<=lambda. Since w<t gives D<lambda, F_N<0
at r=1 and at every r>=1. Necessarily r<1, proving the preliminary reduction (R).

The remaining constraints can be written without a mixed-energy
abbreviation. In the region (R), a primitive candidate must satisfy

    Tr<=AD,
    T a r(w-1)<=1,
    u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-(u-1)T^2 eta^2>=0.        (14)

The last expression is F_N/u after expansion. Section5 proves these
necessary inequalities are incompatible with the remaining endpoint cut
throughout (R).



## 5. Exact elimination of the remaining asymmetric region

Assume (R), the endpoint cut u<=2T/(T+1), and F_N,F_Q>=0.
Continue to write a=u-1, A=u+1, D=w+1, and put K=T-A.
Thus r=K-D and eta=(K-D)/D.

For fixed u,D in (R), F_N strictly decreases with T>=T_0:
its negative term -a u T^2 eta^2 strictly decreases, and its other
T-dependent term has coefficient u^2(2-u^2)<0.
The quantity

    T a (T-A-D)(D-2)

strictly increases with T there. The endpoint cut says
T>=u/(2-u). Therefore any putative feasible T induces feasible necessary
inequalities at the smaller mass

    T_min=max{T_0,u/(2-u)}.                        (15)

Here and below D is unchanged. At this smaller mass K-D>0 still holds,
because T_min>=T_0, u<7/4, D<lambda, and
T_0-11/4-lambda=t-3/4>0.

For the remainder of this section set T=T_min and K=T-A. Define

    B=u^2+u-1,
    N(D)=a(A^2-T^2)D^2
          +K[2aT^2+u(2-u^2)]D-aT^2K^2,
    Q(D)=T a(K-D)(D-2)-1.                         (16)

Thus N(D)=D^2 F_N/u>=0 and Q(D)<=0. Let

    kappa=(T^2-A^2)/T>0,
    ell=aT^2(K-2)+KB+2aA^2>0,
    c_0=-aK[T^2(K-2)+2A^2]+(T^2-A^2)/T.

The positivity of ell follows from K>2, a>0, B>0. Subtraction of the
quadratic coefficients gives the exact identity

    N(D)-kappa Q(D)=ell D+c_0.                    (17)

Hence D>=D_L:=-c_0/ell. Moreover Q is strictly increasing for every
D<=lambda, since

    Q'(D)=T a(K+2-2D),
    K+2-2lambda>=T_0-u+1-2lambda=2-u>0.

It is therefore enough to prove Q(D_L)>0: then D>=D_L and D<lambda
would imply Q(D)>0, a contradiction.

The switch in (15) is exactly at

    M_0=2T_0/(T_0+1)=1+t/2.

### 5.1 First interval: t<u<=M_0, T=T_0

Clearing the positive denominator ell^2 gives

    ell^2 Q(D_L)
       =-T_0 a c_0^2-T_0 a(K+2)c_0 ell
          -(2T_0 a K+1)ell^2
       =(u-t) P_8(u).                             (18)

Here P_8 is the following degree-eight polynomial, with coefficients
listed in ascending order of powers of u:

    13696+9680t,
    -44720-31632t,
    42848+30300t,
    -4028-2832t,
    -13912-9832t,
    7640+5396t,
    -1664-1179t,
    163+116t,
    -6-4t.

Identity (18) follows by substitution of ell,c_0 and t^2=2; all
quantities in it have been specified explicitly.

For 0<=z<=1 put u=t+(1-t/2)z. Write
B_(d,k)(z)=binomial(d,k) z^k(1-z)^(d-k). Direct expansion gives

    P_8(t+(1-t/2)z)=sum_(k=0)^8 beta_k B_(8,k)(z),

with the following EXACT coefficients:

    beta_0=768+544t,
    beta_1=762+539t,
    beta_2=(5144+3632t)/7,
    beta_3=9573/14+13539t/28,
    beta_4=3044/5+3022t/7,
    beta_5=57287/112+81003t/224,
    beta_6=3131/8+1915t/7,
    beta_7=15827/64+21623t/128,
    beta_8=79+783t/16.

Every coefficient is strictly positive. The Bernstein basis terms are
nonnegative and sum to one, so P_8(u)>0 throughout [t,M_0].
As u>t, equation (18) proves Q(D_L)>0 on this first interval.

### 5.2 Second interval: M_0<=u<7/4, T=u/(2-u)

Put p=u, q=2-u>0, and K_num=p-Aq. Define the explicit polynomials

    ell_num=a p^2(K_num-2q)+K_num B q^2+2aA^2 q^3,
    c_num=-a p K_num[p^2(K_num-2q)+2A^2 q^3]
            +(p^2-A^2 q^2)q^3,
    X=-c_num, Y=p q ell_num.

These are just cleared versions of the same ell and c_0:

    ell=ell_num/q^3,
    c_0=c_num/(p q^4),
    D_L=X/Y, Y>0.

Substitution into Q gives the second exact identity

    q^2 Y^2 Q(D_L)
      =p a(K_num Y-qX)(X-2Y)-q^2 Y^2
      =u(u-2)^3(u^2-2)^2 P_13(u),                 (19)

where

    P_13(u)=
       2u^13-9u^12-16u^11+96u^10+28u^9-428u^8
       +248u^7+480u^6-160u^5-656u^4+256u^3
       +288u^2-64u-64.

All denominator factors are positive; (19) is an ordinary polynomial
identity, checkable by multiplying the displayed polynomials.

We prove P_13(u)<0 on [M_0,7/4] by an explicit sign certificate.
First,

    P_13(M_0)=(-1807+1277t)/64<0,

because 1807^2-2*1277^2=3791>0. Next, the derivative has the exact
Bernstein expansion on [5/3,2]

    29229255 P_13'(5/3+z/3)
       =sum_(k=0)^12 b_k B_(12,k)(z), 0<=z<=1,

where the integer coefficients in increasing k are

    -822569770,
    -1552896015,
    -2651133300,
    -4264197048,
    -6587625132,
    -9878494050,
    -14470817670,
    -20793631500,
    -29391880320,
    -40950087840,
    -56318574240,
    -76541675760,
    -102886977600.

Every b_k is strictly negative, and 29229255>0. Thus P_13 decreases
strictly on [5/3,2], which contains [M_0,7/4]. The asserted negative
sign follows from its value at M_0.

In (19), u>0, (u-2)^3<0, (u^2-2)^2>0, and P_13(u)<0. Therefore
Q(D_L)>0 on the whole second interval too.

Both alternatives in (15) now contradict (16)--(17). This eliminates
(R), so the only possible primitive schedule with T>=T_0 is Silver.

## 6. Attainment and the signed-terminal extension

At h=(t,2,t), the following nonnegative multipliers, with every
unlisted multiplier zero, give an exact identity:

    lambda_01=2,
    lambda_10=2lambda,
    lambda_21=2t lambda,
    lambda_31=lambda_13=2lambda,
    lambda_23=2lambda^2+2t lambda,
    lambda_32=2lambda^3,

    V_h+sum_(i!=j) lambda_ij Q_ij=0.               (20)

Substitute x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i and t^2=2 to verify (20) by matching
each function coefficient and each gradient inner product. For example,
the finite value divergences are (-2t,-4,-2t,4+4t), exactly canceling
the four coefficients of V_h. The remaining gradient coefficients all
vanish. Since Q_ij<=0 and every multiplier is nonnegative, (20) proves
V_h>=0 in every finite dimension, establishing attainment.

If T>=T_0 and h is not Silver, the preceding contradiction shows that
at least one of the explicitly constructed cut, quadratic, one-sided
Huber, or two-dimensional mixed witnesses has strictly negative energy.
All those witnesses have x_3=g_3 and the saturated value formula, so
Q_(i,3)=0 for every i<3. Thus for any real numbers beta_0,beta_1,beta_2,
that same witness satisfies

    V_h-sum_(i<3) beta_i Q_(i,3)=V_h<0.

It also rules out a full PSD intrinsic identity whose only possibly
negative interpolation coefficients are on these incoming-terminal
edges: the signed edges vanish, while every other nonnegative
coefficient multiplies a nonpositive Q. This proves the stronger
necessity statement without a dual-attainment assumption.

## 7. What remains open

The theorem gives exactly

    max_{primitive h of length3} (1+sum h)=(1+sqrt(2))^2,

with a unique maximizer, and the same necessary capacity/equality
restriction under arbitrary incoming-terminal credit. It does not
give the required all-length capacity induction, arbitrary-tail
certificate re-selection, or a constant-preserving connection from
primitive energy to the original normalized objective problem.

At original N=2^m take epsilon=N^-1,eta=N^-2, B with P(B=b)=(1+(1-epsilon)b)/2. Conditional on B=b, draw ONE sign V of mean mu_b, where mu_+=-7/10,mu_-=9/10, and put Ck=V for everyk. This coarse measure has four atoms with probabilities P(B=b)(1+mu_b v)/2. Mix it with coefficienteta of the original conditional-product law37 in9b6c78ae964f4861, using the same B law and the same conditional mean for every Ck. The final character weights are exactly(1-eta)w_coarse+eta w_product, with standard chronological labels and the bijection s<->(B,C). Every finite weight is positive. All first moments stay in the screened chart, but E C1C2=1-eta+eta(49/100+4epsilon/25)>1/2. This avoids a hypothesis of the obstruction; it DOES NOT establish any legal positive nonsaturating amplitude law, one global interpolant, anchor, true polar distance, or useful exact payoff. Those remain the substantive missing tasks and must be derived jointly, including every original remote pair. Signed amplitude/label changes are separate possible routes only with equally explicit hypotheses. No further launch or verifier allocation is inferred.

Complete joint proof attempt d54d97705d464425 contains the independently derived projection law and the following explicit construction; no new fact is assumed. Let K={g:sum g=0,sum_(i in S)g_i<=|S|(d-|S|)/d for every S subset[d]}, and Phi(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2). K is the centrally symmetric convex hull of permutations of a_i=(d+1-2i)/d and contains a relative neighborhood of0. The normal-cone characterization of Euclidean projection gives gradPhi=P_K, Lipschitz constant1, argminPhi=span(1,...,1). For even d>=4 put A_(2j-1)=A_(2j)=(d+2-4j)/d and b_(2j-1)=1/(2d),b_(2j)=-1/(2d). Start x0=gamma A+b,1<gamma<=2. Its true initial distance squared is [4gamma^2(d^2-4)+3]/(12d). The gradient g0=A+b lies in K: its even sorted prefixes equal k(d-k)/d and its odd prefixes are smaller by1/(2d). The residual x0-g0=(gamma-1)A supports K there, since it is constant on each pair and its strict jumps occur only at the saturated even prefixes. Thus this is the actual gradient, not a prescribed jet. These r=d/2 residual blocks are distinct and nondegenerate.
For enclosing m>=3,0<tau<=1/4 put lambda=1+sqrt2,beta=1-tau/m,c=lambda(1+tau/m),alpha=beta/c. Use H0=(),H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+alpha*c^j,H_j) with the SAME parameters in every child and n=2^m-1. The first true step gives x1=(gamma-1-alpha)A-alpha b. Both A and b are in K, and |gamma-1-alpha|+alpha<=1, so x1 is in K. For the frozen gamma2 every pair reverses; adjacent sorted pairs remain separated by(4-5alpha)/d. Thus genuine nonlinear rank motion occurs before entry into the quadratic core.
At physical index t the step is1+alpha*c^(nu_2(t+1)). Counting binary valuations gives prefix product P_i=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s_2(i)), where s_2 counts binary ones. Hence sigma_i=P_i/P_1=(-1)^(i-1) beta^(i-1)c^(1-s_2(i)) has absolute value<=1 for EVERY i>=1. Central symmetry proves the exact remaining path x_i=sigma_i*x1 in K, g_i=x_i, Phi(x_i)=||x_i||^2/2. All ordered interpolation residuals are Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0 by the same projection normal condition; in particular none of the remote or incoming-terminal rows is deleted.
The resulting payoff is V=J beta^(2n-2)c^(2-2m), where J=[4(gamma-1-alpha)^2(d^2-4)+3alpha^2]/[4gamma^2(d^2-4)+3]. The same-word scalar unit quadratic has Qunit=beta^(2n)c^(-2m), so V/Qunit=J/alpha^2. The numerator difference has sign (gamma-1)(1+alpha)[(1-alpha)gamma-(1+alpha)]<0 whenever alpha>1/3 and1<gamma<=2. In the stated domain alpha>=11/(13lambda)>1/3, since20>13sqrt2. Thus V<Qunit for every such d,m,tau,gamma. The same conclusion holds at originaltau1,m>=10 via alpha>=9/(11lambda)>1/3. Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives B_n<=Z_1 lambda^m, so this actual subfamily has B_n V<=Z_1 lambda^(-m) beta^(2n)(1+tau/m)^(-2m), uniformly over all its dimensions and amplitudes. Its supremum tends0. No bound on unrestricted W and no exclusion of other starts is asserted. All this mathematics remains unverified; TASK forbids fact_submit.

# A concrete affine completion for the variable-matrix repair lane

Status: new self-contained candidate algebra, NOT verifier-accepted. This is
an explicit parameterization for the next proof attempt, not a re-selection
theorem or a strict cone separation. No numerical solver or fact_submit.

## Definitions and full matrix identity

Fix h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0 with sum s>0. All n+1 formal gradients are independent.
Let x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and define the actual Q_ij and intrinsic V_h by

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                               -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

Define the column e of n+1 ones, the lower triangular matrix A with A_ii=1
and A_it=h_t for t<i, and r=A^(-1)e. Thus r_0=1 and
r_(i+1)=(1-h_i)r_i. The vectors e,r are independent because s>0.
Write omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T and

 D_quad=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),
 c_quad=1-(1+s)^2 r_n^2.

For a real off-diagonal multiplier array lambda, put
L=diag(lambda e)-lambda. Value cancellation in V_h+sum lambda Q is exactly
L^T e=omega. By definition L e=0. The COMPLETE formal gradient matrix is

                 M=D_quad+sym(L A),                 (1)

where sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. To check this, the position contribution is
sym(L(A-I)). The squared-difference terms are sym(L)-diag(omega)/2.
Adding the diagonal terms in V_h gives exactly (1).

## Which PSD matrices admit some real multiplier representation?

Claim. A symmetric PSD matrix M has a real multiplier representation (1)
with the actual value cancellation if and only if

                    M e=0, r^T M r=c_quad.           (2)

This permits arbitrary real multiplier signs; the actual sign restrictions
for a primitive or E_theta certificate remain separate and are not deleted.

Necessity: evaluating the value-canceling identity on the affine Huber run
with every gradient equal to1 gives e^T M e=0, hence M e=0 by PSD.
The full-curvature quadratic trajectory has gradients r_i and every Q_ij=0.
Telescoping h_i(2-h_i)r_i^2=r_i^2-r_(i+1)^2 gives
r^T D_quad r=1-(1+s)^2 r_n^2=c_quad, and evaluating the same identity on
that legal quadratic gives the second assertion in (2).

Here is an explicit sufficiency construction. Put C=M-D_quad and set

 U=[e,r], Z=[C e-A^T omega,-C r], H=U^T U, W=U^T Z.

The two-by-two matrix H is positive definite. The matrix W is skew-symmetric:

 e^T C e=omega^T A e=s^2+sum h_i^2,
 r^T C r=0,
 e^T(-C r)+r^T(C e-A^T omega)=-omega^T A r=-omega^T e=0.

The first equality uses M e=0 and the displayed diagonal D_quad, and the
second uses r^T M r=r^T D_quad r. Define

 K_0=Z H^(-1)U^T-U H^(-1)Z^T
                          -U H^(-1)W H^(-1)U^T.     (3)

It is skew-symmetric, and multiplication by U gives K_0 U=Z: the last
two correction terms cancel because Z^T U=-W. Now put

 B=C+K_0, L=B A^(-1), lambda_ij=-L_ij for i!=j, lambda_ii=0.

The relations K_0 r=-C r and K_0 e=C e-A^T omega give
L e=B r=0 and L^T e=A^(-T)B^T e=omega. Thus L is precisely the row
Laplacian of its extracted off-diagonal array, and its flows cancel every
actual value coefficient. Also sym(L A)=C, proving (1).

All representations of this same M arise by replacing K_0 with K_0+S,
where S is any skew-symmetric matrix satisfying S e=S r=0. Indeed their
difference must be skew after multiplying L by A, and must kill both e,r;
the converse follows from the same equations. This also recovers directly
the fixed-remainder obstruction already independently proved earlier.

Consequently a PSD real-multiplier identity with no sign restrictions exists
if and only if c_quad>=0. For sufficiency, let
z=r-e(e^T r)/(e^T e), so z!=0 and r^T z=||z||^2. The rank-one choice

                 M=c_quad z z^T/(r^T z)^2

satisfies (2); use (3). The case c_quad=0 allows M=0. This consequence is
NOT a smooth-convex validity theorem: unrestricted negative multipliers can
invalidate such an implication. It merely separates the full PSD condition
from the indispensable actual interpolation sign inequalities.

## Concrete next interface for four positive steps

If n=4 and the schedule is quadratic-balanced, c_quad=0, every permitted PSD
matrix must annihilate both e and r. The space orthogonal to span{e,r} has
dimension3. Thus M is an arbitrary PSD three-by-three matrix on that space,
and the free S in (3) has three independent skew entries on that same space.
All twenty actual multipliers are then EXPLICIT affine functions of these
six symmetric and three skew coordinates, through (3).

The actual E_theta requirements remain

 lambda_ij>=0 for j!=4,
 lambda_i4>=-theta h_i for i<4, 0<=theta<1.

Ordinary primitivity requires lambda_i4>=0 as well. A successful repair must
move the symmetric M coordinates, not just S: the earlier unit-step family
already refutes fixed-M repair in arbitrarily many positive steps. No change
to M is declared PSD merely because its scalar quadratic calibration is
correct; all three eigen-directions in the displayed quotient must remain
PSD and every multiplier sign must be checked.

The next concrete attack is to start on a four-step quadratic-balanced face
and either construct such a simultaneous M/S repair for one negative terminal
edge, or give an exact E_theta identity and a legal negative-V witness for
the same actual schedule. Physical capacity and objective amplification are
additional obligations. This note supplies neither outcome and does not
claim the general cone problem solved. All new algebra is shared research
evidence only; no fact was added to the graph.
